**Dinghai day.** Day Nobility, with the Three Wonders complete. Communication with the month’s qi and a strong body gives distinction. Mao and Wei completing the triple Seal combination give great distinction. 〈Another account says:〉 Delay through self-punishment. 〈Recorded cases—**Yihai / Jiaxu:** a censor. **Wuzi / Jiazi:** a provincial graduate. **Guichou / Yichou:** an associate prefect. **Bingzi / Wuyin:** distinguished. **Dingchou / Renzi:** an imperial relative; one account says that Zi and Chou in year and month join Hai in an unbroken sequence, making Officer and Killings heavy, yet Ding’s yin and yielding nature alone finds this most auspicious. **Wuwu / Gengshen:** first in the provincial examination. **Bingchen / Guisi:** Li Zongyan, deputy chief minister of a court. **Gengxu / Jiashen:** Zhu Yibin, assistant surveillance commissioner, who withdrew to care for his parents through their remaining years. **Yimao / Jichou:** “Jia ministry” [office uncertain].〉

〈The Kun Fish Transforming into the Peng configuration:  
From this, renown shines forth.  
Luck travels Officer-and-Lu ground;  
Could such advancement be ordinary?〉

> Ding, the Heavenly Origin, meets its Hai hour;  
> The level road to azure clouds stretches far.  
> Ambition fulfilled, old feathers shed, the rooster becomes a phoenix;  
> The Peng’s course lies open for ten thousand li of flight.

> A Ding day approaches Xinhai hour;  
> Heavenly Origin, Lu and Horse in the Same Country.  
> A metropolitan graduate in office dons the robes of court:  
> Surely a general or chancellor from a humble house.  
> A worthy wife and filial children are assured;  
> Imposing bearing and authority beyond measure.  
> Swift promotion to the censorate, ministries, and capital offices  
> Comes from Wealth and Officer at Birth and flourishing.[^137]

## 49. Wu Days, Renzi Hour
### 六戊日壬子時斷
<!-- source: L2142-L2181 -->

> A Wu day born at Renzi hour:  
> The month communicates with the four seasonal ends, Wealth within the Tomb.  
> If the body combines and transforms into true Fire,  
> In flourishing Water luck, guard against harm to the eyes.

On a Wu day at Renzi hour, wife and Wealth both flourish. Wu takes Ren as wife and Wealth; Ren Water flourishes at Zi, while Wu Earth lacks qi. Birth in Chen, Xu, Chou, or Wei month without transformation obtains wealth. But if Wu combines with Gui and transforms, then falls into flourishing Water’s country, Fire cannot display its light. Undertakings come to nothing, empty rather than substantial, and eye affliction is to be expected. Strength through communication with the month’s qi gives distinction.

**Wuzi day.** Wealth Star configuration. Yin or Mao month gives Lu and Horse Turning toward the Origin and manifest distinction. You, Hai, or Chou in year and month, with northwest luck, brings gold and purple and censorial office. Yimao year is avoided for punishment; Guisi month gives punishment amid flourishing; Bingwu month likewise brings punishment amid flourishing. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingchou / Bingwu:** Vice Minister Yang Wei. **Yihai / Jichou:** a deputy surveillance commissioner. **Bingchen / Xinmao:** a metropolitan graduate. **Renxu / Guimao:** a chancellor. **Jiazi / Bingzi:** a commander-in-chief. **Gengwu / Yiyou:** palace third. **Gengzi / Wuyin:** a metropolitan graduate.〉[^138]

**Wuyin day.** Mao month gives distinction through Direct Officer. Summer is adverse; autumn offers little smooth passage. Chou or You in year and month is auspicious. Jisi month is avoided for punishment, Jihai month for ominous punishment. 〈Recorded cases—**[Bingzi] / Dingyou:** Vice Minister [Hong Chaoxuan], as transmitted in the original text. **Renyin / Guimao:** a prefect. **Jiawu / Yihai:** a deputy court director. **Wuxu / Renxu:** a censor. **Guiwei / Jiayin:** Surveillance Commissioner Liu Kai. **Gengshen / Jimao:** a deputy chief minister of a court. **Dingwei / Dingsi:** an associate prefect. **Jiayin / Bingyin:** a gentleman appointed through family privilege. **Jiawu / Bingzi:** a censor.〉[^139]

**Wuchen day.** Spring’s flourishing Wealth and Officer give distinction. Summer is adverse; autumn offers little smooth passage. Winter strengthens Wealth; southwest luck gives fifth or sixth rank. Gengchen month brings ominous self-punishment; Xinsi month, Road-Cutting and ominous punishment; Yichou month, ruin and misfortune. 〈Recorded cases—**Renshen / Renyin:** Liu Tao, right censor-in-chief. **Bingzi / Xinmao:** a prefect. **Jimao / Yihai:** a mobile-force commander. **Yisi / Gengchen:** a privy councillor. **Xinwei / Gengzi:** a chancellor. **Dingchou / Yisi:** a metropolitan graduate. **Gengwu / Wuyin:** a metropolitan graduate. **Wuchen / Bingchen:** a capital court minister. **Bingyin / Gengzi:** a censor.〉

**Wuwu day.** Hour and day clash, threatening wife and children. Si or Wu in year and month gives censorial office; Yin, Mao, or Hai a place among the Six Ministers. Shen month with Wood-and-Fire luck gives a marquis or earl. Bingwu month threatens bodily mutilation; Guihai gives self-punishment and early death. Unmixed Zi and Wu in year and month, or Chou and Xu again in punishment, give great distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Renzi / Guichou:** a senior provincial administrator. **Wuwu / Renzi:** a senior provincial administrator. **Wuxu / Guihai:** a senior provincial administrator. **Renxu / Renyin:** a deputy chief minister of a court. **Gengzi / Yiyou:** an associate salt-transport commissioner. **Renxu / Xinhai:** a provincial administration commissioner.〉

**Wushen day.** A high fate: obstruction first, flourishing afterward. Zi or You in year and month gives distinction through Indirect Wealth upon the Hour. Si, Wu, Wei, or Xu allows restraint and transformation of the body and exclusive employment of Wealth: all are auspicious. Shen or You makes Food or Hurting Officer generate Wealth; Jia or Yi exposed in the heavenly stems gives both wealth and distinction. Jisi month is avoided for punishment and harm, Renwu for early death and injury, Guihai for solitude and poverty. 〈Recorded cases—**Xinwei / Bingshen:** Grand Secretary Liang Chu. **Yisi / Yiyou:** a commander-general. **Yiyou / Yimao:** a commander-in-chief. **Yisi / Jiashen:** a prefect. **Wuzi / Xinyou:** a prefect. **Bingwu / Bingshen:** a metropolitan graduate. **Gengwu / Dinghai:** an assistant surveillance commissioner. **Gengwu / Bingxu:** a bureau secretary. **Jiyou / Dingmao:** a tribute graduate who became a prefect. **Guihai / Jiazi:** a senior capital official.〉

**Wuxu day.** Yin, Si, or Wu in year and month removes Wealth and retains Seal, giving third or fourth rank. 〈The classic says:〉 “Seeing righteousness, one can forget profit: choose Seal and relinquish Wealth.” Zi or You gives fifth-rank Hanlin office. Pure You with Water-and-Wood luck gives the highest distinction in the Jade Hall. Wuwu month brings facial tattooing and early death; Xinmao, ruin and ominous punishment; Guichou, punishment; Gengwu, poverty and blindness. 〈Recorded cases—**Renzi / Jiyou:** Zeng, palace first and academician. **Guihai / Jiazi:** Chief Censor Tan Shishan. **Jiayin / Wuchen:** a senior provincial administrator. **Jichou / Wuchen:** deputy chief minister of Imperial Sacrifices. **Guimao / Xinyou:** an official in Personnel administration. **Wuyin / Guihai:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jihai / Bingzi:** a metropolitan graduate. **Dingwei / Yisi:** a provincial graduate, subjected to punishment.〉

〈Flourishing Wealth Generating Officer:  
Talent and ability stir the four quarters.  
Missing the time, one lives in a humble room;  
Purpose fulfilled, one becomes marquis or prince.〉

> A Wu day rejoices to meet Renzi hour;  
> A strong body and flourishing Officer are fittingly matched.  
> In luck, turn away from the ground of Rest and Confinement;  
> With honor and emolument, go forth and act at will.

> A Wu day meets Renzi hour;  
> This is flourishing Wealth generating Officer.  
> “Transformed into Ding, Ding stretches boundlessly”—  
> Leaving you to wander and flee east and west.  
> In flourishing Wood luck, manifest advancement;  
> Literary accomplishment of many graceful kinds.  
> Widely read, free to pursue delight,  
> Rich and distinguished, with wealth of ten thousand strings.[^140]

## 50. Wu Days, Guichou Hour
### 六戊日癸丑時斷
<!-- source: L2182-L2217 -->
〈The unfavorable months for the six Wu days below are all judged as above; an offense in the hour is judged likewise.〉

> A Wu day born at Guichou hour:  
> It turns away to follow the wife and form a pair.  
> The person is deft in nature and very intelligent,  
> Especially fond of romance, flowers, and wine.

On a Wu day at Guichou hour, Wu takes Gui as wife and Wealth. Chou contains Gui’s residual qi; following Wealth, Gui flourishes. Communication with the month’s qi transforms it into true Fire and gives intelligence. Communication with Earth qi gives wealth, distinction, and command of authority. Summer birth with eastern luck brings manifest distinction.

**Wuzi day.** Birth in [Xu] month gives Mixed-Qi Seal-Ribbon. Bing or Ding exposed in the heavenly stems brings wealth and distinction without requiring another named configuration. If year and month are Wu and You and the stems expose Geng and Yi, Yi combines with Geng, and Wu with Gui. Wu takes Geng as Food; Geng flourishes at You and has Noble Person at Chou. Gui is Wealth, Yi Officer, and Seal roots in Wu. Day and hour, stems and branches, form Heaven-and-Earth Virtue Combining, with Noble and Lu interwoven. Added Net-Pattern Nobility gives the highest rank. 〈Recorded cases—**Gengwu / Yiyou:** Grand Secretary Wang Ao; one account gives Jiayin hour. **Guimao / Jiayin:** a subprefect. **Yimao / Guiwei:** an associate prefect.〉[^141]

**Wuyin day.** Yin, Si, Wu, Wei, or Xu month gives auspicious Fire transformation. Autumn and winter are ordinary. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiashen / Yihai:** a deputy surveillance commissioner. **Renxu / Wushen:** an associate prefect. **Jiayin / Gengwu:** a servant.〉

**Wuchen day.** Yin, Si, Wu, Wei, or Xu month gives Fire transformation its ground. A Shen–Zi–Chen Wealth configuration is also auspicious. Pure Earth in the year and month stems and branches, completing the Four Storehouses, gives distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Jihai / Dingmao:** Censor Zhao Yao, whose office reached Minister of Personnel. **Renwu / Bingwu:** an envoy.〉

**Wuwu day.** Yin, Xu, Wei, or Wu in year and month gives intelligence, outstanding advancement, and imposing authority. 〈Recorded cases—**Bingyin / Yiwei:** Vice Minister Lin Yingliang. **Wuyin / Dingsi:** a commander-in-chief. **Gengchen / Jichou:** a commander-in-chief. **Guiwei / Bingwu:** a provincial graduate. **Gengxu / Bingxu:** a provincial graduate. **Dingwei / Guimao:** a capital court minister.〉

**Wushen day.** Birth in Chen, Xu, Chou, Wei, or Wu month gives wealth and distinction, with fondness for drink and sex. Summer birth with eastern luck gives distinction. In autumn and winter, Wealth flourishes and generates Officer; but a declining body gives either early death or poverty. 〈Recorded cases—**Jichou / Guiyou:** a prefect. **Gengwu / Guiwei:** Chief Censor Tang Shiji. **Gengxu / Guichou:** rich. **Renshen / Xinhai:** rich.〉

**Wuxu day.** Summer birth with eastern luck gives distinction. Chen, Xu, Chou, or Wei month gives wealth, distinction, and authority, with fondness for flowers, wine, and romantic pleasures. 〈Recorded cases—**Yimao / Gengchen:** a provincial graduate. **Guiwei / Gengshen:** Xie Xuelong, Vice Minister of War, died by throwing himself into a river; much misfortune throughout life. **Wuchen / Yichou:** a Noble Consort.〉

〈Judge the favorable and unfavorable years and months of these six days flexibly and in relation to one another. Compare the recorded fates as well, and the meaning can be obtained.〉[^142]

〈Combining and Transforming into Southern Li:  
Wealth’s gate opens day by day.  
No clash or breaking within the pillars:  
Blessing and emolument come of themselves.〉

> At Chou hour, Wu and Gui combine and transform within the coffer;  
> Most welcome punishment and clash, most fear the lock kept shut.  
> When luck suddenly brings the key that opens it,  
> The household flourishes and a livelihood is made.

> A Wu day meets Guichou hour;  
> It transforms into blazing Fire and gives forth light.  
> Luck through Water’s ground is unfitting;  
> Luck reaching the southeast brings flourishing.  
> Separation from ancestral property leaves one unsettled;  
> Following the wife, one purchases fields and an estate.  
> Otherwise, blood kin suffer injury;  
> Later years bring splendor and abundant flourishing.

## 51. Wu Days, Jiayin Hour
### 六戊日甲寅時斷
<!-- source: L2218-L2273 -->

> A Wu day born at Jiayin hour:  
> In Illness, the body is further injured by ghost.  
> Communication with the month’s qi and bodily strength are auspicious;  
> A declining, weak day stem gives one who dies young.

On a Wu day at Jiayin hour, the body declines and ghost flourishes. Within Yin, Jia has Established Lu, while Bing generates the body. A body-strengthening month, support, and rescue transform ghost into Officer and give wealth and distinction. The reverse gives poverty and early death. Body-strengthening luck is also auspicious.

**Wuzi day.** Ruin first, development and distinction afterward. Wei-month birth with restraint exposed in the stems gives distinction. Wu month without restraint gives intelligence and association with the distinguished. Chen, Xu, and Chou strengthen the body; Hai and Mao strengthen Killings. With Seal and restraint, distinction follows. Pure Yichou in year and month gives gold and purple. 〈Recorded cases—**Renwu / Dingwei:** a provincial administration commissioner. **Jiaxu / Wuchen:** a bureau secretary, not long-lived. **Wuxu / Bingchen:** a county magistrate. **Bingwu / Xinchou:** a county magistrate. **Bingshen / Jihai:** a censor. **Wuyin / Renxu:** a deputy commissioner.〉[^143]

**Wuyin day.** The body is strong; blood kin do not share one dwelling. Wu month gives Blade and Seal carrying Killings; Zi month, Direct Wealth siding with Killings, with restraint exposed in the stems. Hai–Mao–Wei gives pure Killings and a yielding body, with Blade exposed in the stems. Southwest luck brings military command over ten thousand li. Si, Chou, or Xu in year and month strengthens the body to withstand Killings; You, Chou, or Shen restrains Killings. All give great distinction. Robbery Sha controlling the body is disliked and ominous. 〈Recorded cases—**Gengzi / Wuzi:** a minister. **Renzi / Renzi:** a chief censor. **Gengyin / Renwu:** a minister. **Guimao / Jiwei:** a vice minister. **Gengxu / Yiyou:** a metropolitan graduate. **Xinhai / Wuxu:** a senior provincial administrator. **Xinchou / Gengzi:** Vice Minister Cai Ang. **Yiyou / Guiwei:** a commander-in-chief. **Wuxu / Renxu:** Commander-in-Chief Ma Gui. **Jiawu / Wuchen:** a provincial graduate. **Dingyou / Bingwu:** a provincial graduate. **Jihai / Dingchou:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jiaxu / Xinwei:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jiawu / Wuchen:** a marquis. **Jiwei / Wuchen:** Gu Tianjun, palace third.〉

**Wuchen day.** Indirect Officer upon the Hour, Seal-Ribbon carrying Killings. Geng or Xin restraining it in the pillars gives distinction. Chen, Xu, Chou, or Wei strengthening the body also gives distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuzi / Bingchen:** a surveillance commissioner. **Jiachen / Guiyou:** a remonstrance official. **Bingwu / Xinchou:** first in the provincial examination and a metropolitan graduate. **Gengxu / Yiyou:** Gu Xiancheng, first in the provincial examination and a metropolitan graduate. **Renwu / Gengxu:** Chief Censor Lu Shude. **Jisi / Gengwu:** Wang Yuanji, vice chancellor of the Imperial Academy. **Jiaxu / Yihai:** He Fengsheng, palace second, entered the Grand Secretariat. **Renzi / Xinhai:** Vice Minister Chen Daoheng. **Bingshen / Gengzi:** a provincial graduate.〉

**Wuwu day.** Day Yang Blade and hour Indirect Officer: Blade combining with Killings gives distinction. Si, You, or Chou month gives astonishing skill with the clerk’s knife and brush; restraint must not be excessive. Yin, Mao, Chen, Si, Xu, or Zi in year and month, with restraint among the heavenly stems, gives great distinction. 〈One account says:〉 Transforming into distinction means rising from low station to rank. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuwu / Guihai:** Tang Xiaochun, probationary Hanlin scholar in the Jichou examination year; died that same year. **Wuxu / Guihai:** Zhang Yuanbian, palace first. **Xinsi / Gengzi:** Vice Minister Wang Xilie. **Xinyou / Gengyin:** Vice Minister Zou Shouyu. **Yiwei / Wuyin:** a chancellor. **Dingsi / Wushen:** a provincial graduate. **Yiyou / Guiwei:** military distinction. **Gengchen / Wuwu:** rich. **Jimao / Gengwu:** Zhang Lun, bureau deputy director, entered through tribute selection; a man of learning and conduct.〉[^144]

**Wushen day.** Shen or You gives Hurting Officer carrying Killings; regions of substantial Earth give distinction. Chou and Mao in year and month remove Killings and retain Officer, giving great distinction. Metal-and-Fire luck is needed to reach the highest rank of a subject. 〈Recorded cases—**Yichou / Jimao:** Ge Shouli, left censor-in-chief, an eminent minister. **Xinmao / Gengzi:** Chief Censor He Dongxu. **Wuwu / Renxu:** Han Yinglong, palace first in Yiwei, died in Dingyou, short-lived. **Xinchou / Xinchou:** a provincial graduate. **Jiayin / Gengzi:** literary distinction. **Jiazi / Xinwei:** military distinction. **Yihai / Xinsi:** a deputy surveillance commissioner. **Gengyin / Renwu:** a palace official.〉

**Wuxu day.** Chen, Xu, Chou, or Wei month with Water-and-Wood luck gives third rank. Chou or Wei in year and month with Metal-and-Water luck gives wealth and distinction together. Shen, Zi, or You in year and month gives distinction and flourishing descendants. Si and Wu give distinction but injury to wife and children. 〈Recorded cases—**Gengchen / Bingxu:** a minister. **Xinyou / Yiwei:** a censor. **Jiaxu / Jiayin:** Academician Yang Manzhi. **Dingwei / Dingwei:** Chief Censor Liu Xun. **Yichou / Yiwei:** a prefect. **Renchen / Guichou:** a subprefect. **Renxu / Guichou:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jiaxu / Dingmao:** a provincial graduate. **Bingchen / Jiawu:** a provincial graduate. **Dingsi / Renzi:** a provincial graduate. **Wuxu / Jiayin:** a metropolitan graduate and deputy surveillance commissioner. **Yichou / Gengchen:** Minister Wan Yukai. **Dingwei / Gengxu:** a Noble Consort of the Eastern Palace. **Bingwu / Jihai:** a ministry bureau director.〉[^145]

〈Indirect Officer upon the Hour:  
A strong body welcomes Seal in attendance.  
Without punishment, clash, control, or breaking,  
One holds the twin powers of general and chancellor.〉

> Yin hour with a Wu day is inherently no common thing;  
> It rises, outstanding, beyond the world’s multitude.  
> A humble house will surely produce a general or chancellor;  
> But with a weak body, hardship follows.

> A Wu day at [Jiayin] hour is the proper form;  
> The body strong, flowers and trees encounter spring.  
> Indirect Officer encountered fears punishment and clash;  
> Borrowed Killings becomes instead the seal of authority.  
> Strong Killings and a declining body diminish blessing;  
> It is hard to rely on siblings and parents.  
> Should luck travel Seal’s course, blessings come together;  
> Talent and renown find an auspicious beginning.[^146]

## 52. Wu Days, Yimao Hour
### 六戊日乙卯時斷
<!-- source: L2274-L2317 -->

> A Wu day born at Yimao hour:  
> It is well for Hurting Officer not to appear in the Four Pillars.  
> Neither Xin nor Jia encountered, neither clash nor harm:  
> Achievement and renown may be assured into old age.

On a Wu day at Yimao hour, Officer is strong and the body weak. Wu employs Yi as Officer. At Mao, Yi flourishes and Wu dies. Without support or communication with the month’s qi, a weak body turns Officer into ghost: even with distinction, life is short. Communication with the month, a strong body, and support, with no Shen, You, Xin, or Geng to injure Officer and no Jia Wood to break the fate, give manifest distinction.

**Wuzi day.** Hour and day punish one another, injuring wife and children. One succeeds and establishes oneself independently. Repetition in year and month gives censorial office. Wu and You completing the Four Cardinal Positions give great distinction. 〈The *Shenbai Classic* says:〉 A Fire-and-Wood image gives distinction and few years. 〈One account says:〉 Ominous punishment. 〈Recorded cases—**Bingwu / Dingyou:** Minister Ma Wensheng, first rank, with a completed nine-year assessment; an eminent minister of his age. **Yisi / Dingmao:** a prefect. **Jiachen / Yihai:** a metropolitan graduate. **Dinghai / Renzi:** Hanlin scholar Wu Kexing. **Yisi / Jimao:** a deputy surveillance commissioner.〉

**Wuyin day.** Hai or Zi in year and month gives a worthy wife, filial children, and distinction. Pure Mao and Chen are auspicious; Yin and Si are also auspicious. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiazi / Gengwu:** an academician. **Wuchen / Yimao:** a bureau director. **Yihai / Wuyin:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jiachen / Dingchou:** a salt-transport commissioner. **Wuxu / Yichou:** Yuan Jixian, governor of Yun; an ominous outcome. **Wuzi / Yichou:** Wu Kongjia, palace third. **Guiwei / Jiayin:** a provincial graduate. **Gengzi / Guiwei:** a graduate of the Dingmao provincial examination, met calamity in Renwu.〉

**Wuchen day.** Solitary but distinguished. Si or Mao in year and month gives a strong body and flourishing Officer, honoring oneself and making the ancestors illustrious, with filial children and a worthy wife. 〈The *Shenbai Classic* says:〉 A Water-and-Wood image gives distinction without longevity. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuchen / Yimao:** Minister Yan Maoqing; Two Stems Unmixed; he sided with the wicked and harmed the good, and was banished to frontier service. **Renxu / Guimao:** a prefect. **Xinwei / Bingshen:** a prefect. **Bingchen / Yiwei:** a censor. **Xinhai / Bingshen:** a metropolitan graduate. **Renshen / Gengxu:** a bureau deputy director. **Jiashen / Gengwu:** a surveillance commissioner. **Gengchen / Yimao:** a deputy surveillance commissioner. **Dingwei / Gengxu:** a chancellor. **Wushen / Gengshen:** a chancellor. **Bingshen / Wuxu:** Luo Wanhua, palace first and minister.〉[^147]

**Wuwu day.** A Blade configuration: Direct Officer removing Blade brings blessing. Zi, Yin, Mao, Chen, Wu, Wei, Shen, or Hai in year and month all give manifest distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuchen / Jiazi:** Grand Secretary Zhao Zhenji, unyielding and upright, died in the fourth month of Bingzi year. **Jiwei / Bingzi:** Zhou Yan, left censor-in-chief. **Bingwu / Bingshen:** a censor. **Renshen / Renzi:** a senior provincial administrator. **Yimao / Wuyin:** a metropolitan graduate. **Dingwei / Bingwu:** distinguished. **Guihai / Renxu:** distinguished. **Guiyou / Dingsi:** a censor.〉

**Wushen day.** Loss of the ancestral establishment in middle life. Spring gives distinction; summer association with the distinguished; autumn solitary hardship; winter wealth and distinction together. Si month with Wood-and-Fire luck gives distinction at upper fifth rank. 〈Recorded cases—**Renwu / Dingwei:** a chancellor. **Yihai / Xinsi:** a commander-in-chief. **Renyin / Gengxu:** a county magistrate. **Dingyou / Dingwei:** the Prince of Ning.〉

**Wuxu day.** Spring strengthens Officer; summer supplies both Officer and Seal: manifest distinction. Autumn is ordinary, winter distinguished. Xu or Chou in year and month gives third-rank provincial office. Xu month with eastern luck gives solitude and poverty. 〈One account says:〉 A humble origin. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuzi / Renxu:** an assistant surveillance commissioner. **Dingsi / Jiyou:** a county magistrate. **Dingchou / Wushen:** a provincial graduate. **Guimao / Jiazi:** a judicial commissioner. **Gengwu / Guiwei:** a ministry bureau director. **Yiyou / Wuyin:** “dissolute and alone” [caption uncertain].〉[^148]

〈Heavenly Lu Turning toward the Origin:  
The Jade Rabbit reaches the Palace of the Toad.  
Without punishment, clash, or breaking,  
The pool’s dragon transforms and finds passage.〉

> The hour brings Officer, seated upon Lu and authority;  
> A Wu day encountering it is no ordinary fate.  
> Only bodily strength and support complete distinction;  
> Restrain or bind Officer Star, and distinction too is difficult.

> A Wu day meets Yimao hour;  
> Wood’s clash and Sixfold Combination open passage.  
> Golden Rooster and Jade Rabbit display their glory;  
> Joined hands hold the shining seal of authority.  
> Yi, You, and Xin injure the ranks of geese;  
> Flowers bloom again before fruit is formed.  
> The stranded dragon finds Water and rejoices to soar through clouds;  
> When luck arrives, it rises above the multitude.[^149]

## 53. Wu Days, Bingchen Hour
### 六戊日丙辰時斷
<!-- source: L2318-L2367 -->

> A Wu day born at Bingchen hour:  
> Treasure hidden in Wealth’s Storehouse benefits the body.  
> Without injury or breaking, what further need to ask?  
> Lu and Horse assist one another: a person rich and noble.

On a Wu day at Bingchen hour, Officer is hidden in Wealth’s Storehouse. Wu employs Ren and Gui as Wealth, Yi Wood as Officer, and Bing as Inverted Food. At Chen, Bing Fire lacks qi, Ren and Gui enter the Storehouse, and Yi retains residual qi. Support and communication with the month’s qi give manifest distinction.

**Wuzi day.** Spring birth gives association with the distinguished; summer hardship; autumn high authority but a shortened life; winter Wealth. Communication with Fire and Earth and support give distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Xinyou / Bingshen:** a Celestial Master. **Xinyou / Gengzi:** a prefect. **Yihai / Jiashen:** Jiang Jinhe, palace third, not long-lived. **Gengxu / Wuzi:** Grand Secretary Wu Daonan. **Renyin / Yisi:** Chief Censor Wang Yishi. **Dinghai / Xinhai:** minor distinction. **Guimao / Yichou:** blind in both eyes.〉

**Wuyin day.** Dragon Singing and Tiger Roaring: great distinction in middle life. Zi month with northern luck gives third-rank office in regions of substantial Earth; eastern luck gives censorial office. Chou or Chen in year and month gives fourth or fifth rank. Yin, Wu, or Xu year, a month communicating with Earth qi, and Wood-and-Fire luck give the highest rank. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingsi / Dingwei:** a duke. **Guiyou / Bingchen:** a bureau secretary. **Guihai / Bingchen:** great wealth.〉

**Wuchen day.** Injury to the father and great advancement. Communication with a body-strengthening month, followed by northeast luck, gives wealth. A Water-or-Wood month with body-strengthening luck gives distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Jisi / Wuchen:** a metropolitan graduate; a commander-in-chief shared the fate. **Jiayin / Guiyou:** Lin Yanghao, deputy censor-in-chief. **Yichou / Yiyou:** a provincial administration commissioner. **Gengwu / Jiashen:** a chancellor. **Gengxu / Jimao:** Lin Cai, a capital court minister. **Gengxu / Yiyou:** Minister Wang Yingjiao.〉

**Wuwu day.** Hai or Mao in year and month with northeast luck gives wealth and distinction. Wei or Chou with southwest luck gives censorial office. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiazi / Yihai:** Hanlin Compiler Fan Se. **Xinwei / Xinchou:** a censor. **Xinwei / Bingshen:** rich, with many sons. **Gengwu / Wuyin:** a subprefect. **Gengyin / Renwu:** a commander-in-chief. **Wuwu / Guihai:** Vice Minister Chen Suoxue. **Jimao / Renshen:** rich. **Jimao / Jiaxu:** rich.〉

**Wushen day.** Chen, Xu, Chou, or Wei month gives distinction. Even without communication with Earth qi, Earth luck gives distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Gengzi / Bingxu:** Liu Yuanzhen, Minister of Rites, without sons. **Wuwu / Guihai:** Su Maoxiang, governor of Zhejiang. **Wuyin / Yimao:** a tribute scholar.〉

**Wuxu day.** Hour and day clash, threatening wife and children. Kuigang with northeast luck gives weighty authority and developing blessing. You month gives association with the distinguished but no blessing or emolument. Hai, Mao, or Wei month is judged through Officer and Seal and gives distinction. 〈One account says:〉 Great wealth after ruin. 〈Another says:〉 Great misfortune and punishment. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiaxu / Xinwei:** Minister Sun Jiao. **Guihai / Xinyou:** Vice Minister Li Shian. **Gengwu / Dinghai:** a metropolitan graduate. **Guihai / Yimao:** a provincial graduate. **Wuchen / Gengshen:** a military commandant. **Gengxu / Gengchen:** distinguished. **Dingchou / Renzi:** rich. **Bingshen / Xinchou:** the Prince of Lu.〉

〈Wealth Hidden in the Water Storehouse:  
Without a clash, the Storehouse does not open.  
Let the key release the lock,  
And at the extreme of Obstruction, Peace returns.〉

> Bingchen hour encountered is Inverted Food;  
> The Storehouse holds Wealth and Officer behind a locked door.  
> Without the key, advancement is difficult;  
> Exactions and toil carry one through morning and evening.

> A Wu day at Bingchen hour is the proper form;  
> Fire’s light seated in the Storehouse accomplishes nothing.  
> Wealth and Officer, locked away, promise flourishing;  
> Await only the key, then take them into use.  
> Mao and Xu release Yi and Gui;  
> Wealth, distinction, and renown rise high.  
> Luck through Fire and Earth brings no free passage;  
> Undertakings seem wholly a drunken dream.[^150]

## 54. Wu Days, Dingsi Hour
### 六戊日丁巳時斷
<!-- source: L2368-L2409 -->

> A Wu day born at Dingsi hour:  
> Seal generates, and the day’s Lu rejoices to return to the hour.  
> No Wealth or Officer, no punishment, clash, or breaking:  
> Early comes the season when wind and cloud meet.

On a Wu day at Dingsi hour, Seal-Ribbon meets Lu. Wu’s Lu dwells at Si; Ding is Seal, and Ding Fire reaches Imperial Prosperity at Si. Wealth and Officer should not appear in the year and month stems or branches: Officer damages Lu, Wealth damages Seal. Without Officer or Killings, avoiding Wealth-and-Officer luck and traveling Food, Hurting Officer, or Seal luck gives high distinction. Punishment, clash, breaking, and harm are avoided.

**Wuzi day.** Distinguished. Zi month gives Direct Wealth and advancement in regions of substantial Earth. Hai or Chou in year and month gives the highest office; among inner-court officials, both wealth and distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Xinhai / Xinchou:** Yang Rong, Junior Preceptor, an eminent minister. **Jiazi / Bingzi:** a metropolitan graduate. **Yimao / Renwu:** a privy councillor. **Xinhai / Jiawu:** great wealth. **Bingyin / Jihai:** an official in Personnel administration.〉

**Wuyin day.** Yin and Si punish one another, threatening wife and children. Wu month with northeast luck gives censorial office. Yin or Wu in year and month gives fourth rank. 〈Recorded cases—**Renwu / Renyin:** a vice minister. **Wuyin / Dingsi:** an assistant surveillance commissioner. **Guiyou / Xinyou:** Academician Zhang Bangji. **Jiyou / Jiaxu:** Chief Censor Xu Minshi; a provincial graduate who became a subprefect shared the fate. **Gengyin / Wuyin:** a provincial graduate. **Bingshen / Jiawu:** Chief Censor Lian Biao.〉

**Wuchen day.** Si, You, or Chou month gives a romantic disposition and weighty authority. No Wealth or Officer in the year and month stems or branches opens the road to azure clouds. Hai or Xu with southern luck gives fifth or sixth rank; Shen with northern luck gives distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiazi / Bingwu:** a minister. **Xinmao / Xinchou:** a vice minister.〉

**Wuwu day.** Without Jia, Yi, Mao, or Yin in the pillars, this is the Day’s Lu Returning to the Hour. Moreover, Wu’s Lu occupies the hour and Ding’s Lu the day: Exchanging Lu, giving high distinction. Hurting Officer, Food, and Seal luck are auspicious. With Jia, Yi, Yin, Mao, or Wei, employ Officer and Seal instead; this too gives distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Guiyou / Yichou:** Minister Guo Yongbin. **Jichou / Dingmao:** a junior provincial administrator. **Bingxu / Guisi:** a senior provincial administrator. **Renxu / Jiyou:** distinguished. **Bingchen / Bingshen:** service in both provincial commissions. **Jiaxu / Jisi:** an ominous outcome.〉

**Wushen day.** Spring and summer are ordinary; autumn brings successes and failures; winter wealth and distinction. Wood-and-Metal luck is auspicious. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiaxu / Bingyin:** a vice censor-in-chief. **Xinmao / Gengyin:** a deputy surveillance commissioner. **Dingyou / Dingwei:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jichou / Bingyin:** a provincial graduate. **Jiwei / Wuchen:** received an honorific patent through a distinguished son. **Wuchen / Gengshen:** a distinguished son and immense wealth. **Guiwei / Dingsi:** violent death. **Guimao / Jiayin:** a bureau secretary. **Renxu / Jiachen:** deputy chief minister of Imperial Sacrifices. **Jiyou / Jisi:** a metropolitan graduate.〉

**Wuxu day.** Without clash or breaking, without Wealth or Officer in year and month, and without Wealth-and-Officer luck, distinction follows. Yin month is judged as Indirect Officer, giving distinction in Wei and Shen luck. Zi month with Jia exposed also gives distinction. Hai–Mao–Wei is auspicious even with Wealth and Officer: the Returning-Lu configuration does not avoid Officer, Killings, and Wealth here, for a strong body obtaining them finds blessing instead. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingwei / Guimao:** Vice Minister Lin Wenjun. **Guisi / Yimao:** Minister Zeng Tongheng. **Yihai / Gengchen:** a bureau secretary. **Jiayin / Guiyou:** a metropolitan graduate, died early.〉[^151]

〈Wind and Cloud Gathering Together:  
Officer Star should not appear.  
No injury or Defeat in year and month:  
The road to achievement lies open throughout.〉

> The day stem’s branch-Lu rejoices to return to the hour;  
> Without Wealth and Officer, distinction may be expected.  
> No breaking at Yu’s Gate, the three-tiered waves:  
> Watch one leap carry it into Heaven’s Pool.

> A Wu day approaches Dingsi hour;  
> Lu Origin and Seal-Ribbon meet.  
> Early it plucks the crimson cassia and enters the Toad Palace;  
> Joined hands hold the authority of dukes and ministers.  
> Food God and Hurting Officer luck are auspicious;  
> Wealth and Officer encountered bring no achievement.  
> Parents and siblings do not join in support;  
> If breaking occurs, seek another configuration to employ.[^152]

## 55. Wu Days, Wuwu Hour
### 六戊日戊午時斷
<!-- source: L2410-L2447 -->

> A Wu day born at Wuwu hour:  
> The person is fierce, the nature often unyielding.  
> Transformation into Fire within the month favors office;  
> Breaking, harm, punishment, and clash are good instead.

On a Wu day at Wuwu hour, Blade Spirit is repeated. Wu takes Wu [午] as Blade, giving fierceness and great firmness. Punishment, clash, breaking, or harm restraining Blade, with communication through the month’s qi, gives frontier military office. Transformed qi in the month obtaining the season gives great distinction. Jia in the year or month stem makes a monk’s or Daoist’s fate. Fire flourishing in the year burns Wu Earth, often bringing spleen or lung disease. Even if the month does not communicate with Yin, Wu, Xu, or Hai, communication through luck also gives distinction.[^153]

**Wuzi day.** Si, Yin, or Xu month gives censorial office. In summer it is not smelted and cast: punishment first, development afterward. Flourishing ground brings much official trouble and loss of the ancestral establishment. 〈Recorded cases—**Gengshen / Bingxu:** Salt-Transport Commissioner Zhang Guo. **Bingwu / Xinmao:** first in the provincial examination.〉

**Wuyin day.** Xu month completes the triple Fire configuration; Gui exposed in the stems transforms, and obtaining season and ground gives distinction. Shen and Zi in year and month assemble Water; Yin and Wu assemble Fire: the subtlety of Achieving Completion. Chen month also attains it. You gives Hurting Officer, Wei Officer and Seal; both are auspicious. 〈Recorded cases—**Guiwei / Xinyou:** Wang Zi[mian], vice censor-in-chief. **Wuchen / Jiayin:** a bureau deputy director. **Wuwu / Jiwei:** a metropolitan graduate. **Renshen / Renzi:** a supervising secretary; a military commandant shared the fate. **Jiazi / Wuchen:** a provincial graduate. **Guiyou / Wuwu:** violent death. **Gengzi / Jichou:** Li Tongfang, high in the metropolitan examination.〉[^154]

**Wuchen day.** Arching Lu. Without Yin, Si, Jia, or Yi in year and month, distinction follows. Mao or Shen gives military office. You, Chou, or Hai with southern luck gives distinction. 〈One account says:〉 Ominous punishment. 〈Recorded cases—**Guimao / Guihai:** a prefect. **Bingyin / Guisi:** a prefect. **Jiachen / Guiyou:** a censor. **Guiyou / Guihai:** a censor. **Guiwei / Wuwu:** a county magistrate. **Jimao / Dingchou:** a provincial graduate.〉

**Wuwu day.** Punishment first, development afterward; often no good end. Yin, Si, Wu, or Xu in year and month gives great distinction through Seal-Ribbon. Pure Wu gives military office, imposing authority, and great renown in command of a region. Hai, Mao, Wei, or Shen gives both wealth and distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Wushen / Wuwu:** a minister. **Wuyin / Wuwu:** a chancellor. **Yisi / Renwu:** Vice Minister Zhang Chao. **Renyin / Xinwei:** a surveillance commissioner. **Yihai / Guiwei:** a metropolitan graduate. **Renwu / Dingwei:** a senior provincial administrator. **Xinmao / Gengzi:** a chief minister of a court. **Renchen / Jiyou:** a subprefect. **Xinmao / Gengyin:** a provincial graduate. **Renzi / Renzi:** a capital court minister. **Wuchen / Wuwu:** an ominous outcome. **Guiwei / Wuwu:** solitary, poor, and blind.〉

**Wushen day.** Arching Nobility. Yin in year and month with southern luck gives censorial office. Wu gives distinction and longevity; Xu literary distinction. Wei filling the position and Si’s Established Lu linked in one continuous sequence give great distinction. 〈One account says:〉 Death under the law, or otherwise in battle. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingmao / Gengxu:** Shen Lian, a registrar, harmed by the powerful chief minister and put to a violent death. **Yisi / Jiashen:** a senior provincial administrator. **Dingwei / Guiwei:** distinguished. **Yihai / Xinsi:** an imperial prince. **Renwu / Yisi:** rich.〉[^155]

**Wuxu day.** Great misfortune. Mao or Wu in year and month with Officer luck gives manifest distinction. Yin gives Seal-Ribbon carrying Killings, changing misfortune into good. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiayin / Gengwu:** a prefect. **Xinsi / Xinmao:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jiyou / Xinwei:** a provincial graduate. **Guichou / Renxu:** rich.〉

〈Jade Hidden within Stone:  
Firm and decisive, able to act.  
The Six Relations are impaired and incomplete;  
Wife and children suffer many early losses.〉

> Wu meets Wuwu in Fire’s country, Li;  
> Blade flourishes, the body is strong, and renown shines forth.  
> Luck obstructed, the time adverse, the name remains unmade;  
> Ordinary subsistence carries one through the years.

> A Wu day meets Wuwu hour;  
> Companions delay Wealth and Lu.  
> Repeated wives and children late, however one strives;  
> Advancement comes when luck reaches Ren and Gui.  
> Parents and siblings offer little benefit;  
> The Six Relations are estranged, like ice and burning coals.  
> Only with clash, control, or breaking does it become exceptional;  
> Middle and later years promise distinction.

## 56. Wu Days, Jiwei Hour
### 六戊日己未時斷
<!-- source: L2448-L2485 -->

> A Wu day born at Jiwei hour:  
> Yang Blade and Indirect Officer do not fear a clash.  
> The person’s nature is often fierce,  
> Yet lifelong subsistence need not be ill-fated.

On a Wu day at Jiwei hour, there are Yang Blade and Indirect Officer. Wu takes Ji as Yang Blade and Jia as Indirect Officer. The hour has two Ji, one visible and one concealed, as Blade; Jia Wood combines within Wei’s configuration. Punishment, clash, breaking, or harm restraining Blade and Killings gives distinction. Communication with the month’s flourishing qi gives ordinary subsistence; within twenty years both parents are lost. Even without communication with the month’s qi, obtaining Yin and Shen gives distinction.[^156]

**Wuzi day.** Hour and day pierce one another, threatening wife and children. Early hardship, later good fortune. A month communicating with Earth qi, followed by Wealth-and-Officer luck, gives distinction. A Water-or-Wood month with body-strengthening luck gives distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingchou / Xinhai:** a provincial graduate who served in both provincial commissions; an ominous end. **Wuzi / Guihai:** Chief Censor Tang Daoheng.〉

**Wuyin day.** Difficulty first, ease afterward, through a noble person’s assistance: if not distinguished, then rich. Jia exposed in year and month restrains Blade; Wu or Wei in the branches strengthens the body. Killings and Blade manifest together, giving great distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Bingwu / Jiawu:** a minister. **Gengzi / Dinghai:** Li Duojian, high in the metropolitan examination, bureau secretary in Personnel. **Jiwei / Bingyin:** a probationary Hanlin scholar, transferred to the Ministry of Rites. **Jiashen / Bingzi:** a provincial graduate.〉[^157]

**Wuchen day.** Spring or winter birth with northern luck gives wealth and distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiazi / Xinwei:** Yan Pu, chancellor of the Imperial Academy; his unyielding nature invited calamity, and he died from anger. **Gengyin / Xinsi:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jimao / Renshen:** distinguished. **Wuchen / Jiwei:** distinguished.〉

**Wuwu day.** Yang Blade and Indirect Officer give resourcefulness and planning but a shortened life; otherwise wife and children are difficult to sustain, with repeated establishment and rebuilding. Wu or Hai in year and month with Metal-and-Earth luck gives distinction; Shen or You gives wealth. 〈Recorded cases—**Yimao / Jiashen:** a senior provincial administrator. **Bingyin / Yiwei:** Vice Minister Lin Yingliang. **Jiashen / Gengwu:** distinguished. **Yiyou / Dinghai:** distinguished.〉

**Wushen day.** Distinguished; Wealth-and-Officer luck develops blessing. Yin clashing with Killings is disliked and ominous. One case, **Xinmao / Gengyin**, indeed suffered misfortune through the clash with Killings. 〈Recorded cases—**Guihai / Xinyou:** Vice Minister Liu Boyue. **Wuwu / Dingwei:** a chancellor.〉

**Wuxu day.** Spring’s flourishing Officer gives distinction; summer’s Seal security; autumn ordinary fortunes; winter solitary hardship. Xu month gives fifth or sixth rank through Kuigang. The years and months must form their respective arrangements: Shen–Zi–Chen assembles Wealth, Yin–Wu Seal-Ribbon, Si–You Hurting Officer, Hai–Mao Officer and Killings. Only then is the result auspicious. 〈Recorded cases—**Renzi / Jiachen:** Chief Censor Wang Shou. **Bingyin / Gengzi:** a deputy surveillance commissioner. **Jiyou / Jisi:** a metropolitan graduate. **Renyin / Dingwei:** a bureau director. **Renshen / Wuxu:** a bureau secretary.〉

〈An Ancient Mirror Polished Anew:  
Within darkness, light appears again.  
If luck travels Officer-and-Seal ground,  
Advancement is no ordinary thing.〉

> Within Wei, Wu and Ji Earth gather in a heap;  
> Punishment, harm, and clash arrive, yet affairs find harmony.  
> Dark first, bright afterward: misfortune turns to good;  
> A noble person raises one from the dust of obscurity.

> A Wu day approaches Jiwei hour;  
> The Six Relations and blood kin grow distant.  
> Fire Seal’s hidden support is welcome;  
> Jia, Yi, Yin, and Mao are taken as principal.  
> Flowers fade again, open, and bear fruit;  
> Parents and siblings face early death and solitude.  
> One acts, establishes oneself, and plans alone,  
> Relying on the help of a noble person.

## 57. Wu Days, Gengshen Hour
### 六戊日庚申時斷
<!-- source: L2486-L2525 -->

> A Wu day born at Gengshen hour:  
> Food God in the stem rejoices to draw near.  
> Without Mao, Yin, Jia, or Bing,  
> Why fear that the jade belt will not honor the person?

On a Wu day at Gengshen hour, Wu takes Geng as Food and Ren and Gui as Wealth. At Shen, Geng flourishes, Ren is born, and Wu Earth has qi. Without Bing Fire seizing Food and damaging Wealth, and without Yin or Si to punish and clash, autumn birth gives great distinction. Bing Fire, Jia, Yin, and Si make a changeable person, though achievement and renown may still occur. With only Jia exposed in the year and month stems, the Three Wonders, and the earthly branches assembling a configuration—or even only Yin and Chen—distinction follows.

**Wuzi day.** Birth in Hai or Zi month likewise gives distinction through Combining Lu. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiwei / Renshen:** a chancellor. **Renshen / Renyin:** a minister. **Xinwei / Wuxu:** a prefect. **Guisi / Guihai:** a bureau secretary. **Gengwu / Xinsi:** a provincial graduate.〉

**Wuyin day.** Ominous punishment, followed by development. Birth in Yin month is judged as Food and Lu Restraining Killings, giving distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Bingchen / Xinchou:** a metropolitan graduate. **Wuzi / Renxu:** a senior provincial administrator. **Renshen / Xinhai:** a prefect. **Jiaxu / Bingzi:** a county magistrate. **Jiyou / Jiaxu:** Hanlin scholar Wang Kentang, compiler of medical works including *Standards of Diagnosis and Treatment*, without sons; Xiang Dexiang, a provincial graduate and county magistrate, shared the fate but had sons. Wang came from Jintan, Xiang from Shangyuan.〉[^158]

**Wuchen day.** Exclusive Food and Combining Lu. Pure Yin in year and month gives two Yin unafraid of one [Shen] clashing; this too gives distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiaxu / Gengwu:** Minister Gao Yao. **Renyin / Jiyou:** a prefect. **Renchen / Dingwei:** a prefect. **Wuxu / Bingchen:** a Grand Preceptor. **Jiwei / Wuchen:** the Prince of Shu. **Wushen / Jiayin:** distinguished. **Gengyin / Wuyin:** distinguished. **Gengwu / Wuzi:** distinguished. **Jiyou / Guiyou:** a metropolitan graduate. **Xinsi / Bingshen:** a metropolitan graduate. **Yiyou / Renwu:** the Prince of Liao.〉[^159]

**Wuwu day.** Wei, You, or Chou in year and month, with no Bing, Jia, Mao, or Yin in the pillars, enters Combining Lu; northwest luck gives distinction. With Jia and Bing, it instead fits Spirits Concealed and Sha Submerged and the Four Pillars Entirely Yang: great distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiaxu / Bingzi:** chief minister of Imperial Entertainments. **Wuchen / Jiazi:** a bureau deputy director. **Renwu / Jiyou:** an official in the heir apparent’s household. **Jiazi / Guiyou:** a bureau secretary. **Dingyou / Xinhai:** a provincial graduate. **Gengshen / Gengchen:** a county magistrate. **Xinwei / Guisi:** Vice Minister Bi Maokang.〉

**Wushen day.** Body and Lu Share One Nest: wealth and distinction together. Zi, Chen, Wei, or You in year and month with western luck enters Combining Lu and approaches upper third rank. Chou and Xu in year and month are auspicious; Chen and Xu bring injury and ordinary fortunes. Hai and Si in year and month with northeast luck give first or second rank. 〈Recorded cases—**Renxu / Yisi:** Chief Censor Hu Shouzhong, died violently. **Renshen / Guimao:** the Prince of Yi, sent to confinement within the high walls. **Yihai / Xinsi:** Minister Wang Chonggu. **Jiayin / Wuchen:** Lü Zhen, palace first. **Jiwei / Guiyou:** a chancellor. **Gengshen / Dinghai:** a censor. **Yihai / Guiwei:** a chief secretary. **Renchen / Jiachen:** first in the provincial examination. **Dingchou / Renzi:** a metropolitan graduate. **Xinsi / Bingshen:** Provincial Administration Commissioner Shao Mei, with two sons who were metropolitan graduates.〉[^160]

**Wuxu day.** “Calamity and intermittent fever within” [wording uncertain]. Zi month likewise gives Combining Lu and third-rank literary distinction. Mao or Chen in year and month gives great distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiwei / Bingzi:** a vice minister. **Dingsi / Renyin:** a school instructor. **Wuxu / Gengshen:** Hanlin scholar Han Siwei. **Renxu / Bingwu:** a ministry bureau director. **Dingwei / Guichou:** a filial son.〉[^161]

〈Food God at Birth and Flourishing:  
Hemp clothing is exchanged for brocade.  
The official path favors advancement and employment;  
Name and profit naturally become exceptional.〉

> A Wu day’s hour rejoices to see Geng;  
> Food God Combining Lu promises flourishing honor.  
> If punishment or clash appears amid strength,  
> Only an ordinary livelihood may be allowed.

> A Wu day encounters Gengshen hour;  
> Birth and flourishing upon the branch are rare and excellent.  
> Food God generates [blessing], displaying brilliance;  
> Flourishing qi flows freely above and below.  
> Bing injures the branches and harms the leaves;  
> Jia and Yin diminish the ranks of geese.  
> Without clash, breaking, punishment, or control,  
> There is distinction with piled jade and heaped gold.[^162]

## 58. Wu Days, Xinyou Hour
### 六戊日辛酉時斷
<!-- source: L2526-L2569 -->

> A Wu day born at Xinyou hour:  
> Hurting Officer and Sudden Defeat are feared in the hour.  
> Even with Wealth Star’s assistance in the pillars,  
> Children do not flourish, and life ends early.

On a Wu day at Xinyou hour, the body is defeated and Hurting Officer present. Wu takes Yi as Officer and Xin as Hurting Officer. At You, Xin Metal flourishes and Wu Earth is at Bath, giving pride in nature and low conduct. Yi exposed in year or month brings a hundred calamities. With Yi present, further luck through flourishing Officer brings inauspicious punishment and harm. But a month communicating with generating qi and northern luck gives either distinction or wealth.

**Wuzi day.** Si, You, or Chou month gives Hurting Officer with Officer Completely Removed. Flourishing Wealth luck gives military or censorial office. 〈Recorded cases—**Renwu / Guimao:** a subprefect. **Guiwei / Wuwu:** a gentleman appointed through family privilege.〉

**Wuyin day.** A month communicating with Earth qi and northwest luck gives wealth even without distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Renshen / Renzi:** Minister Wang Xishi. **Jiashen / Bingyin:** a bureau secretary. **Wuchen / Dingsi:** a provincial graduate. **Yimao / Wuyin:** a provincial graduate.〉

**Wuchen day.** Chou or Wei in year and month, with Wealth exposed in the stems, gives distinction. Guisi month places Literary Hall and Study Hall in the monthly framework, indicating literary achievement and high examination success. A Yin, Shen, Si, or Hai year makes the hour offend Disorder Sha: later life bears no fruit. 〈Recorded cases—**Xinsi / Guisi:** Qian Fu, palace first; another account gives Jiayin hour. **Guiwei / Yichou:** a vice minister. **Yihai / Xinsi:** distinguished. **Renwu / Renzi:** Li Daiwen, Minister and Director-General of River Conservancy. **Wuxu / Yichou:** an ominous outcome.〉[^163]

**Wuwu day.** Spring and summer are ordinary. Autumn gives Hurting Officer with Officer Completely Removed, bringing authority and distinction but a shortened life. Winter makes wife and children difficult to sustain. 〈Recorded cases—**Yiwei / Gengchen:** a chancellor. **Bingchen / Gengzi:** a provincial graduate. **Wushen / Jiwei:** Chief Censor Zhou Kongjiao. **Guihai / Yimao:** Vice Minister Qi Guangzong.〉

**Wushen day.** Xu, Hai, Chou, or Zi in year and month gives authority and distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Dingsi / Gengxu:** palace first. **Xinhai / Xinmao:** a school instructor. **Yihai / Xinsi:** a provincial graduate who became a prefect. **Renshen / Jiyou:** Tang Ruji, palace first. **Guisi / Xinyou:** sided with the wicked and met an ominous end.〉

**Wuxu day.** Chen, Xu, Chou, or Wei month gives distinction. The ancients held that Water and Earth reach Defeat at You; consequently, fulfillment is often lacking. Achievement may end in early retirement, or offspring fail to flourish. Look at the preceding six days: none has the fate of a great official. This shows that encountering Hurting Officer in the hour is inauspicious. 〈Recorded cases—**Bingwu / Xinchou:** a vice minister. **Dingwei / Yisi:** a metropolitan graduate. **Jiachen / Jiaxu:** a metropolitan graduate. **Yisi / Gengchen:** a subprefect. **Jisi / Xinwei:** Censor Wu Zhenying, sentenced to death but spared. **Wuzi / Renxu:** a checking official, died while traveling, aged thirty-nine. **Bingshen / Xinmao:** a provincial graduate.〉[^164]

〈Hurting Officer upon the Hour:  
Later years indicate distress and poverty.  
Only when luck travels Wealth-and-Seal ground  
Does a prosperous passage begin.〉

> Wu day meeting Xin is called Stripping Officer;  
> Its occurrence in the hour is especially unwelcome.  
> Officer Star encountered brings extraordinary calamity;  
> Fear above all illness and children lost or lacking.

> A Wu day approaches Xinyou hour;  
> Hurting Officer does not welcome Officer Star.  
> Only luck in Wealth’s country opens passage;  
> Temperament and feelings are unsettled.  
> Without breaking, ancestral property is hard to obtain;  
> The ranks of geese each take flight alone.  
> Repeated wives and children late precede peace;  
> This fate is difficult first and easy afterward.[^165]

## 59. Wu Days, Renxu Hour
### 六戊日戊戌時斷〔壬戌〕
<!-- source: L2570-L2613 -->

> A Wu day born at Renxu hour:  
> The body occupies its proper seat and encounters Heavenly Wealth.  
> Autumn birth communicating with bodily strength  
> Gives a household’s ten thousand strings beyond all doubt.[^166]

On a Wu day at Renxu hour, an exclusive seat encounters Wealth. [Wu] takes Ren as Wealth; at Xu, Water is at Cap and Belt. In spring and summer, Ren Water lacks qi and wealth does not flourish. Winter makes it too strong: Earth is empty, the body weak, and unable to command it. Only after autumn arrives is Wu Earth firm and substantial, with Wealth and Fate possessing qi, giving riches and distinction.[^167]

**Wuzi day.** You, Shen, or Xu month gives wealth filling the household. Chen and Chou clashing or punishing the Storehouse open it: all are auspicious. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiashen / [Jiaxu]:** a deputy surveillance commissioner. **Xinmao / Renchen:** a Grand Mentor. **Gengchen / Jichou:** minor distinction. **Xinhai / Renchen:** a gentleman appointed through family privilege.〉

**Wuyin day.** Yin, Wu, Xu, or Chen in year and month with Metal-and-Water luck gives great distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuwu / Bingchen:** a minister. **Wuwu / Yimao:** a metropolitan graduate. **Guihai / Jiayin:** an assistant prefect. **Dingmao / Xinhai:** a senior provincial administrator. **Jiashen / Wuchen:** Zhao Zhigao, palace third, entered the Grand Secretariat. **Xinhai / Xinchou:** a deputy prefect.〉

**Wuchen day.** Hour and day clash, threatening wife and children. Regions of substantial Earth give great distinction. Mao month with Fire-and-Metal luck gives middling distinction. Yin, Shen, Xu, or Chou with southwest luck gives the highest distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Yiyou / Wuzi:** a censor. **Jiashen / Wuchen:** an associate salt-transport commissioner, without sons. **Xinhai / Renchen:** a censor. **Renyin / Jiyou:** a provincial graduate.〉

**Wuwu day.** Yin, Wu, or Xu month, with much Fire in the hour and year, is judged through Seal-Ribbon. Solitude and injury are not escaped; difficulty comes first, ease afterward. If not distinguished, then rich. Mao month gives auspicious Officer Star restraining Blade. 〈Recorded cases—**Jisi / Guiyou:** a prefect. **Xinhai / Xinmao:** a provincial graduate. **Dingchou / Guimao:** a provincial graduate. **Gengyin / Jiashen:** a duke.〉

**Wushen day.** Spring gives distinction; summer ordinary fortunes; autumn wealth and distinction; winter ordinary fortunes. 〈Recorded cases—**Xinmao / Gengyin:** Vice Minister Zhang Lian. **Renchen / Jiachen:** a provincial graduate. **Jisi / Jisi:** Educational Commissioner Yue Hesheng. **Xinhai / Renchen:** a prefect.〉

**Wuxu day.** Autumn gives wealth and distinction. Summer with western luck, spring with northern luck, or winter with southern luck all give distinction. Chen and Chou months are especially auspicious. 〈Recorded cases—**Gengzi / Jiashen:** a bureau secretary. **Jiazi / Bingzi:** a provincial administration commissioner. **Bingyin / Gengzi:** a provincial graduate. **Guiyou / Yichou:** a provincial graduate, poor and without sons. **Wuwu / Xinyou:** Minister Wang Ji. **Xinchou / Xinmao:** a violent man, executed by dismemberment.〉[^168]

〈Discarding the Old and Welcoming the New:  
Indirect Wealth is encountered in the hour.  
If Companions do not appear,  
A strong body promises free passage.〉

> At Renxu hour, Wealth lies buried in the Storehouse;  
> To open it, one must await the key.  
> Luck travels the Birth and flourishing of Wealth and Officer:  
> Wealth, distinction, and splendor need not be doubted.

> A Wu day meets Renxu hour;  
> Mao and Chou in the pillars bring joy.  
> Zi, Chen, and Yi bring distinction and display Wealth and Officer;  
> Going forth or staying, one has an eminent person’s company.  
> Open the Storehouse, fill a vacant chamber, and join a household;  
> Without clash, the ranks of geese are hard to keep whole.  
> Poverty first, wealth afterward: affairs come to fulfillment;  
> Even without rank, storehouses and chests are richly filled.[^169]

## 60. Wu Days, Guihai Hour
### 六戊日癸亥時斷
<!-- source: L2614-L2652 -->

> A Wu day born at Guihai hour:  
> Transformation into Fire lacks Xu and battles in Water’s country.  
> If Yi, Geng, and Ding appear and Chou is absent,  
> It becomes instead no ordinary fate of office.[^170]

On a Wu day at Guihai hour, Wu combines with Gui and transforms into Fire. Fire reaches Extinction at Hai and battles in Water’s country, so the image does not form. The person has refinement without substance, often belonging to the diverse arts and skilled occupations associated with the distinguished; eye disease is indicated. Wu takes Jia as ghost and Ren and Gui as Wealth. At Hai, Ren flourishes, Jia is born, and Wu Earth’s qi is extinguished. Wealth gathers and disperses. Flourishing Yi, Ding, and Geng appearing in year and month give the distinction of the Three Wonders. A month communicating with Earth qi, a strong body, and Fire-and-Wood luck are auspicious.

**Wuzi day.** With no Wu in year and month to break the configuration, distinction follows. Shen or Zi in year and month with southeast luck, or Hai or Mao with southern luck, gives distinction. Flourishing Si, Wu, and Wei give great distinction. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuchen / Jiwei:** Chief Censor Li Wenjin. **Jiashen / Yisi:** a provincial administration commissioner. **Guihai / Jiazi:** an assistant surveillance commissioner. **Jimao / Gengwu:** Wu, palace first.〉[^171]

**Wuyin day.** Wu or Wei month gives Fire transformation assembling its configuration: a high fate. Spring mixes Officer and Killings but provides warmth, food, and clothing. You or Chou in year and month gives third-rank capital office. 〈Recorded cases—**Yichou / Dinghai:** Minister Zhang Shunchen. **Jiazi / Dingchou:** a surveillance commissioner. **Jiazi / Gengwu:** chief minister of Imperial Entertainments. **Wuchen / Guihai:** a metropolitan graduate. **Xinchou / Renchen:** a metropolitan graduate. **Gengchen / Bingxu:** a metropolitan graduate and county magistrate, grasping and lawless, executed under the law. **Renchen / Guimao:** an honorifically titled gentleman. **Xinmao / Gengyin:** a metropolitan graduate. **Xinwei / Renchen:** an imperial son-in-law. **Renwu / Jiachen:** a censor. **Yihai / Guiwei:** a Noble Consort.〉

**Wuchen day.** Refined. Hai or Zi month gives the Wealth-and-Officer configuration: if not distinguished, then rich. Yin or Mao in year and month gives manifest distinction. Summer birth gives a teaching post. 〈Recorded cases—**Jiayin / Jisi:** a bureau deputy director. **Jiashen / Jiaxu:** a provincial graduate. **Xinchou / Yiwei:** a provincial graduate. **Wuyin / Wuwu:** a metropolitan graduate. **Yiyou / Dinghai:** the honorifically titled Mr. Dai, of an established family in Cangzhou. **Guichou / Jiwei:** Commander-in-Chief Liu Ting.〉

**Wuwu day.** Distinguished. Yin or Si in year and month gives sixth- or seventh-rank distinction in regions of substantial Earth and refined Water. Mao, Wei, Chen, and Chou are all auspicious. 〈Recorded cases—**Renzi / Guimao:** a metropolitan graduate. **Guiwei / Guihai:** a provincial graduate, died young. **Guihai / Yichou:** a metropolitan graduate. **Bingxu / Jihai:** a deputy surveillance commissioner.〉

**Wushen day.** Zi or Wei in year and month gives no ancestral property; wealth comes through the wife. Otherwise, roots are moved and leaves exchanged, with poverty and low station. 〈Recorded cases—**Wuchen / Bingchen:** Chief Censor Xing Shangjian, without sons. **Renyin / Renzi:** a deputy surveillance commissioner. **Jiwei / Dingchou:** a bureau secretary. **Wuzi / Guihai:** a minister. **Xinsi / Gengzi:** a bureau director. **Renyin / Renyin:** Jin Xuezeng, governor of Fujian.〉

**Wuxu day.** Yin, Mao, Wu, Si, Chou, Xu, or Hai in year and month, with Yi, Geng, and Ding exposed in the heavenly stems, gives pure and important office, authority, and distinction. Fire-and-Earth luck is suitable. 〈Recorded cases—**Yiyou / Jimao:** Minister Yao Mo, whose son Lai was palace first. **Xinchou / Xinchou:** a Grand Preceptor. **Bingchen / Gengyin:** a bureau secretary. **Wuxu / Guihai:** a provincial administration commissioner. **Jiazi / Jiaxu:** a prefect without sons. **Jisi / Xinwei:** a censor. **Jisi / Jisi:** a bureau director. **Wuzi / Yichou:** an imperial son-in-law.〉

〈Heavenly Stems Transforming into Fire:  
When the configuration forms, it must be extraordinary.  
Luck traveling auspicious ground  
Makes the name fragrant throughout the four seas.〉

> Wu and Gui transform into Fire, born at Hai hour;  
> Sunset on rivers and lakes darkens, then shines again.  
> Birth in Mao or Wei month gives auspicious Triple Combination;  
> Moving home and changing dwelling must bring peace.

> A Wu day approaches Guihai hour;  
> The heavenly stems’ Fire transformation is exceptional.  
> Flourishing Yi, Geng, and Ding fit auspiciously together;  
> Renown and manifest distinction are assured.  
> Across the four seas, the spring wind brings good cheer;  
> The Six Relations and blood kin suffer injury and loss.  
> A worthy wife and filial children bring contented joy;  
> Without breaking, the examinations yield success.[^172]

*End of Sanming Tonghui, juan eight.*

〈Siku classification: Masters Division; Arts of Calculation; Fate Books and Physiognomy; *Sanming Tonghui*.〉
<!-- source: L2654-L2658 -->

## Translator’s Endnotes

[^01]: **Controlling text and comparison witnesses.** **S** denotes the repository’s [Chinese juan eight](../source/juan-08.md), exported from [Wikisource revision 761697](https://zh.wikisource.org/w/index.php?oldid=761697). The header records its checksum. **G613**, **G614**, and **G615** denote the parallel extracts in the *Gujin Tushu Jicheng*, Arts juan [613](https://zh.wikisource.org/w/index.php?oldid=1942576), [614](https://zh.wikisource.org/w/index.php?oldid=1942577), and [615](https://zh.wikisource.org/w/index.php?oldid=1942578). **U** is the shorter [unlabelled Wikisource juan eight](https://zh.wikisource.org/w/index.php?oldid=845354). These were selectively compared in their rendered forms on 26 September 2026. The G pages transclude separately editable Page texts, so a parent revision alone does not freeze every underlying transcription. These are parallel witnesses, not additional Siku editions, and their agreement does not demonstrate independent descent. S governs coverage and sequence; comparison has not been used to replace S with a composite of all the witnesses.

[^02]: **Reading this reference juan.** A day–hour heading is completed by the year and month in each case; the catalogue is not a list of unrelated two-pillar charts. 身, “body,” usually means the day stem and its capacity to sustain the configuration. 地方 and directional luck frequently refer to the phase-bearing branches, not advice to migrate geographically. Birth, Bath, Coming to Office, Imperial Prosperity, Death, and Extinction are technical stages. Statements about death, disability, wrongdoing, and family injury are historical predictions, not demonstrated properties of people sharing a chart. 官 is translated as Officer when technical, office or official when institutional. Standard unambiguous graphic confusions among 己, 已, and 巳 are resolved by context; consequential or doubtful repairs are noted separately. Wu the stem 戊 and Wu the branch 午 remain distinct, even though their unaccented pinyin is identical.

[^03]: **The source’s heading-level qualification.** The small note links the unfavorable months to the hour under consideration. The compiler later explicitly asks for flexible, contextual judgment. Neither statement licenses treating a named day–hour pair as an unconditional prediction of an actual person’s death. The differing monthly verdicts in the prose and closing verses have not been silently reconciled.

[^04]: **A difficult conditional retained.** S and the comparison texts put the condition “if Ji Earth damages Seal” before communication with the month’s qi. The translation retains that construction rather than deleting Ji or reversing the judgment. The opening poem’s 敗 is the technical Defeat/Bath position, not necessarily the destruction of the entire chart.

[^05]: **Recovering the catalogue’s layout.** Many small-type case lists in S have been flattened from paired rows: a run of years and names is followed by the corresponding months and further annotations. Here they are presented as year/month pairs, with the day and hour inherited from the heading. G supplies useful parallel pairings, but includes some names S omits and omits some cases S includes. Only S’s cases and annotations are translated; unidentified officeholders have not been given names borrowed from G. Malformed strings or uncertain attachments are identified in the relevant notes. These are transmitted examples, not independently verified birth records. Titles are functional English renderings: metropolitan graduate for 進士, provincial graduate for 舉人, and explicit examination placing for 解元, 會元, 狀元, 榜眼, and 探花.

[^06]: **Fu rather than Chuan.** S writes 傳行簡; G613 gives 傅行簡. The reading “[Fu] Xingjian” follows that supported surname repair. It does not add the fuller career information found only in the parallel witness.

[^07]: **The transmitted catalogue has its own textual layers.** The S entry naming the Tianqi emperor is retained, as are later imperial and official examples elsewhere in this juan. Not all such entries occur in G. Attribution of the work to Wan Minying is not an assertion that every catalogue annotation was personally written or observed by him. “From the Central Region” translates 中州人, not a modern citizenship designation.

[^08]: **Names as transmitted.** 陳蕖, 孫錝, and 張我續 are rendered Chen Qu, Sun Zong, and Zhang Woxu according to S’s characters. The translation does not silently replace rare or possibly damaged name-graphs with a conjectured historical identity. 常少 is read as an abbreviated vice-ministerial title in the Court of Imperial Sacrifices. The note that Reader Sun shares Wang Rang’s chart is retained without inventing Sun’s given name.

[^09]: **The final song is not harmonized with the opening rule.** Gengshen, Xinyou, Chou, and Wei appear in a syntactically compressed passage, although the prose excludes several of these from the remote-Lu configuration. S and G613 transmit the tension. “Arched nobility” and the subsequent qualifications are translated in their order; no missing negative has been inserted to produce a uniform rule. 蟾宮折桂 evokes examination success through plucking cassia in the moon’s palace.

[^10]: **An unfinished alternative.** S stops at 主血, “indicates blood,” and G613 does not securely complete it. The translation therefore does not invent a blood disorder, violent death, or specific wound. The apparent contrast between a favorable Metal-Spirit Fire configuration and the next entry’s excessively burned, weak Wood is likewise retained.

[^11]: **Five graduates; an alternative verb.** The Wang Zongmu entry reads 妻夫子五進士: the translation understands five metropolitan graduates among her husband and sons, without inventing their names or asserting five sons in addition to the husband. In the Jiashen judgment S has 化煞, transforming Killings; G613’s rendered text has 純殺, pure Killings. S is retained. Gold insignia and purple robes translate 金紫, an image of elevated official rank, not a separate astrological configuration.

[^12]: **Si, not the Ji stem.** The exclusion 己酉辛丑 is read Si, You, Xin, Chou, with G613’s 巳 supporting the first branch. This is not a list of two complete sexagenary pillars. The entry’s allowance for some You- and Chou-month births remains; its concluding instruction to inspect the exposed stems prevents the prohibition from being absolute.

[^13]: **Two examination dates without a fully specified assignment.** Both the Cao Dazhang and Zhuang Qixian annotations transmit 丙午癸丑科. The two pairs are preserved as named examination dates; the translation does not silently assign one to a provincial sitting and one to a metropolitan sitting where S does not say so. Cao’s note gives 會元榜眼, metropolitan first and palace second; Zhuang’s gives 榜眼. The wealthy merchant sharing Cao’s chart is a separate example of differing circumstances under identical pillars.

[^14]: **Unfinished biographical matter.** Wu Zhongxing’s note has a detached 癸 and 官 amid its flattened columns, after the Dingchou remonstrance and court-flogging reference; their missing connection is not invented. Lu Changgeng’s entry leaves an isolated 人 after the month, without an intelligible place or title. All readable biographical matter is translated, but these remnants cannot responsibly be turned into completed sentences. “Palace official” renders 內官 without adding a personal name or an unrecorded career.

[^15]: **Repeated blessing; differing witnesses.** S repeats 厚福 immediately after the *Shenbai* quotation; that repetition remains in English. G613 gives Hai, Zi, and Mao months in its corresponding preceding sentence, while S has the Seal months Hai and Zi; S’s shorter list governs the translation. 倚頓 is rendered Yi Dun without changing the graph to the better-known form of the name.

[^16]: **Compact branch and stem notation.** The song gives 丁午庚申, which is rendered as Ding, Wu, Geng, Shen in its transmitted order. It could partly refer to a Gengshen pillar, but 丁午 is not a regular sexagenary pair. The Jiazi-specific wording in a song attached to all Jia days is likewise retained. Completion and Incompletion in the Jiachen prose preserve 既濟 and 未濟 and their literary resonance rather than forcing an entirely favorable verdict.

[^17]: **Supported small repairs.** S’s 卯中乙卯 is read “Yi Wood within Mao,” following G613’s 乙木. In the Jiazi verdict S has an anomalous 𠫵 before 有官煞制; G613 has 柱, “the pillars,” supporting that reading. The section’s initial condemnation and later allowance for restrained Killings are both retained.

[^18]: **A malformed year repaired.** S’s 庚甲 beside the violent-death annotation is not a sexagenary year. G613 gives 庚申, Gengshen, followed by Jimao month, supporting the bracketed restoration. The subsequent Jiaxu/Bingyin violent-death case is separate. 林烴 is rendered Lin Jing following the premodern reading: the [Guangyun, juan three](https://zh.wikisource.org/wiki/廣韻_(四庫全書本)/卷3), groups 烴 with 剄 under 古挺切 and glosses it 焦臭. This is a lexical basis for the reading, not independent proof of this particular person’s pronunciation or identity; the graph is not silently replaced with another official’s name.

[^19]: **Cloud enclosure, not a ridge.** The small verse title in S reads 月缺雲隴; G613 reads 雲籠. “[Enclosed] by clouds” follows 籠, a supported repair. The final song’s welcome to Officer/Killings luck sits beside the opening warning about Hurting Officer Meeting Officer; the text’s restraint conditions matter, and no universal reconciliation has been supplied.

[^20]: **A split title.** S has 耿申 followed later by 丞 in the case grid. G613 gives 耿中丞, supporting “Geng, vice censor-in-chief.” This repairs a title, not a supposed Gengshen birth pillar. 金紫 is consistently a rank image; it is not counted among the natal stems and branches.

[^21]: **Punishment and a month variant.** S’s 侵欺間軍 is read with G613’s 侵欺問軍 as sentencing to military service for fraudulent appropriation. The source does not name the irregular tribute student, so G’s supplied name is not imported. In the bureau-director case S gives Yichou/Wuyin, whereas G613 gives Yichou/Bingyin; S’s Wuyin is retained. Pan En’s alternative Jisi hour is the source’s own alternative, not an editorial correction to Wuchen.

[^22]: **An unattached date.** Wuxu appears after Zou Detuan’s Hanlin title before the month row. It is retained as a date with no stated event; it has not been turned into a second month, an examination year, or a death year. The following removal from office and Jiangxi origin remain translated.

[^23]: **Two analytic frames coexist.** The passage moves between ordinary Jia Wood at Illness in Si, Jisi Metal Spirit requiring Fire restraint, and Jia–Ji transformed Earth. These should not be collapsed into one fixed element assignment. 參商 evokes separated stars and hence discord or estrangement among relatives. The *Shenbai* quotation’s favorable transformation and the Jiachen verdict’s unfavorable transformation are both transmitted.

[^24]: **The source explains identical charts geographically.** Fang Fengshi and Zhao Keng share the year/month pair; the note contrasts Chu and Ji through Fire and Water. This is the compiler’s explanation, not verification of geographical causation. G613 also records the comparison. The separate year/month pair for Wu Yingchen is not part of either man’s chart.

[^25]: **Praise and qualification are both retained.** The closing song calls the metropolitan graduate’s name insubstantial, although other parts predict distinction. It gives no license to delete that line. 改祖翻莊 concerns a change in ancestral establishment and homestead, not a specifically documented land transaction. The final 氣象 is a prospect or manifestation; the verse leaves its quality implicit.

[^26]: **A second blood fragment.** S and G613 both transmit 主血發 in the Jiachen alternative. “Blood arising” marks its incompleteness without specifying a disease or outcome. The Lin Ying'ang case preserves the rare name-graph 㭿, read áng in the [Ministry of Education’s Dictionary of Chinese Character Variants, entry C05184](https://dict.variants.moe.edu.tw/dictView.jsp?ID=88114&q=1). The apostrophe separates Ying from ang; likewise Lin Ting'ang later in this juan. These romanizations preserve S’s graphs rather than silently replacing them with an assumed historical identity. The female case in the Jiazi list is the mother’s chart: Zhou Yanru is her son, not the subject whose year and month are given.

[^27]: **Gengshen within the Gengwu section.** Both S and G613 say 午時庚申, literally “Wu hour, Gengshen,” in this poem. The translation retains Gengshen and does not silently replace it with Gengwu or an abstract Metal spirit. The surrounding heading and prose consistently concern Gengwu hour. This is a probable textual problem, not a newly established alternative hour calculation.

[^28]: **A transformed-phase statement.** 金化木, “Metal transforming into Wood,” is explicitly ascribed to the *Shenbai Classic* twice. It is not rewritten as ordinary Metal generating or controlling Wood. These older transformation statements should be distinguished from the standard Jia–Ji, Yi–Geng, and other stem combinations.

[^29]: **Different case catalogues.** G613’s Jiaxu example differs from S’s three entries; S’s Jichou/Yihai, Jihai/Dingchou, and Jihai/Jisi records are all retained without replacing them with G’s named graduate. Weng Zhengchun in the Jiachen list is the son of the woman whose chart is supplied. Repeated year/month pairs in the Jiashen list remain separate recorded cases.

[^30]: **A substantive poetic variant.** S has 不比, “not like,” before the molting fowl becoming a phoenix; G613 has 好比, “just like.” S’s negative is preserved despite making the comparison less straightforward. The preceding small verse is Wealth and Officer Opening the Storehouse in S, but Coming to Office Opening the Storehouse in G. S governs the title.

[^31]: **Hurting Officer, Owl, and two Wu repairs.** The opening poem says 明傷, visible Hurting Officer, while the prose says 明梟, visible Owl; both readings occur in G613 and both remain. In the restraint clause S gives 丙戌 and G gives 丙戊; the latter supports Bing and “[Wu].” The same supported repair is made in the Jiachen verdict. No attempt is made to change every such statement into a uniformly modern Ten-God analysis.

[^32]: **An outcome without a subject.** The Dinghai/Renzi case is labelled only 殺, “killing.” Whether it means killing another, being killed, or an abbreviated Killings judgment is not established here, so the English preserves the bare label. 玉堂金馬 names prestigious courtly/literary precincts and functions as an image of office, not two extra chart stars.

[^33]: **Embryo and Metal Spirit.** The explanation expressly places Jia’s Embryo at You. 胎生元命 is therefore preserved as its own formulation, not replaced with the separately calculated conception pillar. The later Jiashen judgment allows Water to transform Metal’s harmful force and directs the reader away from Metal-Spirit analysis toward Officer and Seal. That change of framework is essential, not a contradiction to be removed by deleting Water.

[^34]: **Reading a damaged character without creating a new rule.** S’s 官煞雨旺 is read as Officer and Killings both flourishing: 雨 is evidently damaged in this context, but no new planetary “rain” influence is posited. The Li Zhi entry retains 誌 as the source’s name-graph; it has not been identified with a different historically better-known Li Zhi. The unnamed wealthy person sharing the chart remains a separate record.

[^35]: **Uncertain names remain uncertain.** S’s 用如盤 is romanized Yong Rupan and marked uncertain; a plausible correction to a different surname or given-name graph is not silently made. Shao Kangjie and Liu Wenzhuang retain the appellations supplied by S, rather than having unprinted personal names inserted. The count of seventeen lives in Luan Shangyue’s note is translated as transmitted, without an independent historical claim about that case.

[^36]: **No missing key supplied.** The sequence 無鑰衝刑開破 is compressed and is also transmitted by G613. The translation retains the lack of a key and the opening/breaking through clash and punishment, rather than manufacturing a new favorable condition. The fowl-and-duck image and the mixed final phase statement are preserved even though their precise technical application is not explained.

[^37]: **“Take precedence” is supported.** S’s 財官都右先 is damaged; G613 gives 都占先, “both take precedence,” supporting the bracketed translation. The title 驛馬天廷 is retained as Traveling Horse at Heaven’s Court. The four directions in the following couplet are not expanded into a migration prescription.

[^38]: **An inherited method, not a single universal rule.** The general verdict uses Hai as Jia’s Birth while also assigning Blade to Yi and Inverted Food to Ren. The final song introduces the Imperial Seat and Purple Tenuity Palace.
